COMMON ORDER:
The issue involved ,r, *":, writ petitions is intrinsically
interconnected and therefore, they a_re taken up and heard
together and are being disposedof by this common order.
Considered the submissions of the lea_tned counsel for the
respective parties and perused the record.
- The petitioners claim to be ttre purchasers of various extents
of plots in Sy.No.2B3 and its sub-dMsions, situated at
AMullapurmet Village, Hayath-nagar Mandal, Ranga Reddy
i
District, through registered sale Qeeds- It is etated tJ,..at disputing
the nature of the lands, the respondent-authorities are. interfering
with the peaceful possession of the petitioners stating that the
said lands werc assigned to various persons and initiating the
summary proceedings under the Telangana Land Encroachment
Act, l9O5 ([or short 'the Act, l9O5J ar}d tlre Telangana Assigned
:_
l^ands (Prohibition of Transfers) Act, ISTT (hereinafter reftrred to
'ROR Act, t977'). The grievanc. if F. petitioners is that the
respondent-authorities are not having any po\rer or authority to
evict them as they have purchased tfre said properties through
registered sale deeds.
A\.
i
i
I

o )
Smt. B. Rachana, Iearned Senior Counst:l appearing for
some of tl.e petitioners vehemently contended that when there are
disputes with regard to classification, identification, localization,
and execution of registered sale deeds in respe(:t of the subject
plots, the respondent-authorities are not havin 3 any power or
authorit5r to evict iire petitioners from the saicl ptots, without
following due process of l.;aw.
Per antra, Sri D.V.Chala.pathi Rao, learned Government
Pleader Tor Assignment, appearing for the responrlents submitted
that as per the l{hasra Pahanis, Serwar, Chesta Pahanis and other
t'evenue records maintained under the provisions; ttre Telangana
Land Revenue Act, 1317 Fasli, the nature of the lands is classified
as- oitancha Sarkari" which denotes as Govt:rnment lands.
karned Government Pleader [urther submitted that the lands
which are recorded as Kancha Sarkari, Gairan, Porambdk and
more paSticularly the grazing land s are not meant for
alienation/allotment either under the provisions o:. the Telangaria
[-and Revenue Act, l3l7 Fasli or Boarcl Standing; Orders or the
poliey enunciated by the State (iovernment uide G.O.Ms.No.1406,
dated 25.07.1958; and the land:; classified as Kanr:ha Sarkari are
intended solely for graziog iind are rescrved for the purpose of
feedrng cattle in villages. It is further submit_ted that aJter

4
conducting socio-economic survey by the Government, if the
petitioners are found eligible for regularization of tlre lands or for
allotment of houses under any Government housing schemes
introduced from time to time, appropriate aclion will be taken to
acconimodate the eligibte perso{rs under t}re relevant schemes.
The case of the petitioners is that the subject lands are
recorded as padavu (uncultivated/fallow) and they have
purchased the same under registered sale deeds and at this length
of time, the respondents a-re not having any right to disturb tlreir
settled legal position by initiating summai-y proceedings under the
provisbns of the Telangana tarrd Encroachment Act, 19O5.
The case of the respondents is tllat as per the revenue
records, the nature of the lands is classifred as Kancha Sarkari
and therefore, the petitioners or their predecessors-in-interest are
not having any title and they are not "ysn sligible for assignment
of the said lands under the provisions of tlre Telangana l^and
Revenue Act, 13 1 7 Fasli or as per the i:olicy issued by the
Government from time to time.
Be that as it may, since it is stated that the petitioners have
purchased the said lands under registered sale deeds without
verifying the classification of thc lands in revenue records and
with a bono ftdc impression that the sellers have valid transferablc

5
title and even if tJle sale deeds relied upon by them ar:e not walid
ones, since all tl1e petitioners are below poverry- line persons and
eligible for allorrnent of house sites as per ttre dir ective principres
of the State policy, whether the petitioners are entitred for
regularization of t,.e said house sites or for accornmodating ttrem
under any of the schemes introduced by the State Government,
this Court is of the opinion that ends ofjustice world be met if the
respondent-authorities are directed to conduct socio-economic
survey and if the petitioners are erigibte for regr.rlari-ation of ttreir
respective plots purchased under registered sale deeds, the
respondents shall c'onsider the same for regu [ariza tio n in terms of
the policy in force. if the petitioners are nor etigiblo and are not in
possession of the subject lands to claim regrrlarization, the
respondents shall follow due process of law beforr: initiating any
action against the petitioners.
With the above observations, these Writ petitions are
disposed of. No order as to costs.
As a sequel, tlre miscellaneous petitions pending, if any,
shall stald closed.
